ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u14	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present to you a draft law amending the Law on Special Solutions for the Implementation of the Budget Law for 2019, the Act on the Company Social Benefits Fund and the Act on the Charter of the Large Family. First of all, this project envisages unfreezing the basis for calculating the social benefit fund in the current year. The Law of 9 November 2018 on specific arrangements for the implementation of the budget law for 2019 establishes the basis for the calculation of social funds from 2013. The current draft foresees that these bases will be from 1 August 2019. These solutions will concern pensioners, former professional soldiers and police officers, Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Counterintelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, Central Anti-corruption Office, Border Guard, State Security Service, State Fire Department, Customs and Carb Services, Prison Service and members of their families. In this case, the draft law proposes that the allocation to the Social Fund should be based on the measures planned in the 2014 Budget Act for pensions. The proposed solutions will also apply to an occupational social benefit fund, i.e. civil workers. For this group of employees, the basis for calculating the write-down will be the average remuneration in the national economy in the second half of 2014, i.e. PLN 3389.90. This means that the basis for calculating the share social benefit fund increases by PLN 111.76 compared to the base of 2013, which results in an increase of 3.41%. It should also be noted that in the case of the budgetary sphere, i.e. Budgetary units of budgetary establishments, expenditure on the occupational welfare fund are compulsory, while for other employers the rules for the creation of the fund depend on the number of persons employed. Another amendment, which is proposed in this bill, is to amend the Bill on the Charter of the Large Family. The proposed provision concerns an amendment of the limits of expenditure adopted for 2019 by the municipalities and the minister responsible for the implementation of this law. families and entitlement to reduced travel by means of public collective transport for parents and spouses of parents holding an important Large Family Charter. Taking into account much more than was assumed in the estimation of the above. limits, interest in the Charter of the Large Family members of multi-child families who had a total of at least three children, the limits adopted will be insufficient to implement the bill in the current year. That's why it's changed. The increase in expenditure related to the implementation of the law by the municipalities will amount to 210 000. PLN, and in the case of production of cards - by 3 million PLN. On the other hand, in terms of entitlement to reduced travel by public collective transport for parents and spouses of parents holding an important Large Family Charter, it will amount to about PLN 8 million. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u16	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the government draft law on special arrangements for the implementation of the budget law for 2019, the Act on an occupational social benefit fund and the Bill on the Charter of the Large Family, print No 3771. The main objective of this law is to unfreeze from 1 August 2019 the social benefit fund in relation to the size set out in the Act of 9 November 2018 on specific arrangements for the implementation of the Budget Law for 2019, commonly referred to as the Around Budget Act. In accordance with the rules in force, the granting of services and benefits and the amount of social benefit fund fees shall be subject to the circumstances of the living, family and material person entitled to receive them. The rules and conditions for the use of social services and benefits shall be laid down by the employer in collective agreements. The proposed unfreeze of social fund write-downs will contribute to support and consequently improve the living conditions of workers and other entitled persons. At this point, it should be recalled that according to the Act on the Social Benefits Fund of 1994 write-downs and increases for a given calendar year, the employer shall transfer to the bank account of the Social Benefits Fund, known as the Fund, within two time limits: until 31 May and 30 September of the calendar year concerned. The proposed law introduces the deadline of 31 October 2019 as the deadline for the payment to the bank account of the fund of additional measures provided for in the proposed law. Art. Points 1 and 2 of this Act amends the amount of the write-down from 1 August 2019 to the social fund for persons entitled to provide pensions to uniformed services and their family members. Art. Point 1 (3) empowers the Minister responsible for public finances to carry out transfers of expenditure between departments, chapters and paragraphs of the classification of expenditure of the State budget in order to finance the expenditure referred to in this draft law. According to Art. 2 of this project is proposed that by 31 July 2019 the fund should be based on the monthly remuneration of the second half of 2013, i.e. the amount of PLN 3278.14 and from 1 August 2019 on the second half of 2014, i.e. the amount of PLN 3389.90. The purpose of this provision is to unfreeze another year of the fund's write-down base starting on 1 August 2019. Art. 3 of this law concerns the amendment of the article. Article 36 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Large Family Charter Act, i.e. changes in the expenditure limits adopted for 2019 by the municipalities and the minister responsible for family matters and the entitlement to reduced travel by means of public collective transport for parents and spouses of parents holding the Large Family Charter. Maximum limit of expenditure from the State budget for the period 2017 to 202028 [[♪ The bell ♪]] For the implementation of the law by municipalities, voivodeships, the Minister responsible for Family Affairs and the Minister responsible for Transport is 763 930 thousand. zł. The Act shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this draft law in the further legislative procedure. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u35	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! It is an extremely important project or two projects jointly considered regarding the new Public Procurement Law. There are a number of problems in the public procurement market. This is largely the result of complex procedures and a very formalised approach to the whole procurement process. There are also disproportionate risks. There is a lack of sufficient control over the performance of contracts resulting from the award of public contracts. The result is a decline in interest in public procurement, a clear reduction in competitiveness, unfortunately also a reduction in the quality of the goods and services delivered and later problems concerning the lives of certain products. Some of these phenomena confirm very important facts. Last year around 50% tenders resulted in the submission of only one offer. In the case of many tenders, these were only two offers. Therefore, it is difficult to say about competitiveness. By law, we want to change these realities of recent times, to significantly improve the efficiency of public procurement. The solutions proposed in the draft law aim to increase competitiveness in the public procurement market and access to the procurement market for small and medium-sized enterprises. The aim is to better manage the entire purchasing process, enhance the role of contracting and contractor cooperation, improve appeal systems, control system, allow identification of strategic contracts and those contracts that are innovative. It is important to improve the procurement procedure, especially in procedures below the EU thresholds. It's interesting, for example. solutions requiring analysis prior to the launch of the procurement procedure in order to achieve the best possible results from the inputs concerned and to select the best methods and means to achieve the objectives pursued. The law allows for precise definition of the principles of cooperation, the conduct of a catalogue of prohibited clauses as regards the content of the contract. It also introduces new rules for valorisation - which is extremely important - pay, as well as the obligation to apply advances or partial payments in contracts over 12 months. Detailed rules shall be laid down concerning the guarantee of proper performance of the contract. The amount of the security shall be determined in percentage to the total price indicated in the tender or the maximum nominal value of the contracting authority's contractual obligation if the tender contains a unit price or unit prices. The security shall be fixed at a rate not exceeding 5% the total price. In order to simplify the conduct of public procurement procedures, the draft law provides for the principle of optionality of a security applicable to all proceedings carried out under the provisions of the law. The appeal procedure will be improved and mediation mechanisms or other amicable solutions will be put in place in the event of disputes. The rules introduced will better ensure the interests of subcontractors. The reduction of court fees on complaints at the second instance will ensure that small and medium-sized enterprises are more accessible to benefit from a complaint against the decisions of the National Board of Appeal. Formalities in non-competitive proceedings will be limited, e.g. in free-hand order mode. The rules on negotiated procedures will be clarified. The project includes solutions for procedures related to the innovation partnership. There will also be a completely remodeled set of solutions in the competition procedures. The solutions of the new law strengthen the role of the President of the Public Procurement Office in preparing and promoting good practices that will support contracting authorities. The drafts were worked in detail at the committee meeting. There have been a number of voices from the social side present at the committee meeting, a number of topics have been submitted for re-examination by the Ministry and by the Public Procurement Office, which will undoubtedly result in amendments today, so that this document - reflecting very intensive work, very many meetings, three-year preparations - is a good document that actually introduces long-awaited solutions so that the whole process is simpler, more transparent, but also competitive, just ensures quality. Given that this work has resulted in a report being prepared and submitted to the High House, I ask that these reports be adopted and that it should continue to be examined - it is about the printing reports 3624 and 3625 - so that, indeed, during this term of office, the Sejm can successfully complete this very large work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u36	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the joint discussion on these items, it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u37	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo! I have the honour to present my position on behalf of the club Law and Justice regarding the Public Procurement Law, which regulates the participation of the public sector in the Polish economy, as well as the provisions introducing this law into the legal system. It is a very important act, it will certainly affect the way and quality of doing business in Poland. The current law was established in 2004, and thus just before Poland entered the European Union, before the huge transfer of public funds. At present, the potential of the current law has been exhausted, and it has ceased to be attractive enough for the private sector to benefit from existing law to the extent that it uses its potential. One of the main identified problems of the current law is the disproportionate share of small and medium-sized enterprises in the public procurement market, so for national proceedings below the EU thresholds, the law introduces a new, easier procedure which does not require procedures based on EU directives. Operators will be able to award contracts using a flexible simplified procedure, which gives the possibility, first and foremost, to negotiate offers to a wider or narrower extent. By introducing solutions beneficial to contractors, this Subcontractor Act is not forgotten. The Act is intended to guarantee them the exercise of all rights exercised by contractors. Contractors were obliged to take appropriate care of the interests of subcontractors, therefore, among others. the law contains provisions that ensure that the provisions of contracts with subcontractors are no less favourable than those of contracts with contractors in terms of contractual penalties, payments or valorisation. Open to small and medium-sized enterprises will be used to change the competition procedure. The competition procedure is simplified, the new competition will allow, among others. for the conclusion of a contract for the performance of a service with which competition work was involved, not only for the design and execution of the project. If we are talking about small and medium-sized companies, it is important to stress the provisions that aim to improve their liquidity, thus reducing the requirements for deposits and advances, as well as the obligation to pay advances and advance payments when the contract goes beyond a period of 12 months. Today, liquidity problems very often make small companies unable to participate in tenders, because they simply cannot afford a security deposit or security. These requirements, which are today, are far too excessive, therefore in the smaller national procedures in the simplified procedure the maximum value of the security which the contracting authority may demand is reduced by half - to 1.5%. In addition, the maximum security that the contracting authority may require shall be 10%. the total price quoted in the tender. The Act introduces a number of conciliation, mediation proceedings, because every settlement is better and more effective than a court order. In addition, the Act proposes solutions for the issue of value-added contracts - that is, a thing that is really very important - and that the price should not be the main condition for consent to the performance of the contract. Important changes also concern the system of legal remedies, namely the reduction of the complaint fee for the ruling of the National Board of Appeal, the extension of the time limit for bringing an action against the decision of the National Board of Appeal. In addition, the Act strengthens the role of the President of the Public Procurement Office in preparing and promoting good practices and model documents. Law and Justice fully supports the bill, but in the course of work in response to a number of suggestions, especially from the social side, we have prepared amendments which I wish to present to your marshal and ask for further proceedings. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This shows the scale of the increases we're seeing on this market. So we want these amendments to be taken into account now, but we do not hide that we will also seek to introduce in the next term of the Sejm provisions which will guarantee the contractors that if there are significant increases in the price of materials and labour, they will be able to apply for the renegotiation of the contract, for valorisation, and that the contracting authority will not be able to oppose such a proposal. Public procurement has a direct and immediate impact on the national economy, including its most important sectors. In many sectors, for every fourth trader, public procurement revenue accounts for half or more than half of the total revenue, and for every tenth - 10% Entrepreneurs - 75% revenue is revenue from public procurement. The current Public Procurement Law does not have a separate classic procurement section below and above the Union thresholds, and the procedures and rules for the award of national contracts do not actually differ from those relating to Union procurement. The proposed amendment proposes that the new law should distinguish and significantly simplify and make the procurement procedure below the Union thresholds more flexible by departing from the procedures applicable to contracts above the Union thresholds and increasing the autonomy of the contracting authority in shaping the procedure for the selection of the economic operator. ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u55	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Act on the Polish Institute of International Affairs, print no. 3689. The subject of the bill is primarily to enable the creation and functioning of foreign branches and foreign subsidiaries of the Polish Institute of International Affairs. The proposed law has a fundamental objective of more effective implementation of the Institute's tasks. This is due both to the need to adapt the relevant legislation to the new challenges in the international arena and to the way similar institutions operate in other European countries. Such a solution justifies the need to equip the Polish Institute of International Affairs with an additional instrument for carrying out statutory tasks, such as analytical and expert support of the most important authorities of the State of the Republic of Poland in the field of foreign policy conduct or promotion of the Polish expert position abroad. Similar regulations work they are already in the process of being introduced in the Czech Republic, Germany, France, the United Kingdom or Spain. The procedated bill assumes the creation by the Polish Institute of International Affairs of local branches with the consent of the President of the Council of Ministers, after obtaining a positive opinion of the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. The director appointed and cancelled by the Director of the Institute will be headed by the local branch. With the consent of the President of the Council of Ministers, PISM will be able to transform and remove local branches after obtaining a positive opinion from the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. The Institute will also be able to establish subsidiaries, in legal and organisational form, depending on their place of establishment, including in the form of a capital company, after obtaining the approval of the President of the Council of Ministers and after obtaining a positive opinion of the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. The subsidiary shall be headed by the Director in the case of the Director of the Institute. Similarly, under similar conditions as in the case of local branches, the Institute will be able to transform and liquidate subsidiaries. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration and some other laws, print No. 3753. As stated at the first reading of this important bill, the purpose of amending the Law of 17 June 1966 is to simplify the administrative procedures for administrative execution, which would benefit not only creditors and enforcement authorities but also the obliged. The main emphasis of the proposed amendments is on the facilitation of enforcement procedures in relation to the obliged person. This concerns changes in the handling of appeals and at the same time the separation of powers between the enforcement authority and the creditor. A specific protection measure has been introduced for the spouse in respect of the execution of the assets of the joint spouses where separate provisions provide for liability with the common property for the monetary claim. In addition, a protection measure has been introduced for the owner of the property or property under the law of the court, which protects the cash claims incurred in the administrative execution. 14 amendments tabled by the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice were considered during the detailed discussion of the various articles of this draft law. Amendment 1 introduced the provision that a statement on the assets and sources of income held shall be made under the strictest criminal liability for the lodging of a false statement. Amendment 2 is based on the adaptation of Article. 26ca for the issue of another electronic instrument. Amendment 3 aims to adapt Article. 67 § 1 to the amendment resulting from Article. 3 Commercial Companies Code Act. Amendment 4 aims to eliminate doubts as to whether in Article. 67 and Article 67 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and The next amendment, amendment 5, is intended to adapt the article setting out the enforcement authorities entitled to carry out checks on the correctness of implementation of the enforcement measure applied by debtors to the amendment consisting of the repeal of Article. 19 § 3. Amendment 6 aims to adapt terminology within a single editorial unit by replacing the words "tax collector" by "worker serving the enforcement body." Amendment 7 is an adaptation of the Article. 164 § 2b to the amendment introduced by the Act of 4 July 2019. Code of Administrative Procedure. Amendment 8 is intended to include in Article. 166c of the Member States of the European Union and third countries in protective proceedings. Amendment 9 aims to take account of the amendments to the Act of 4 July 2019. Amendment 10 aims to remove discrepancies between the provisions of the Enforcement Act and the provisions of the Pension and Pensions Act from the Social Insurance Fund. Amendment 11 is intended to provide access to data from the list of students and PhD students in order to carry out basic tasks by the National Tax Administration. Amendment 12 is to adapt the Act of 4 July 2019 amending the Law on Enforcement in Administration to the changes resulting from the draft of the Act presented. Amendment 13 aims to ensure that the amended provisions can be applied to the co-execution of administrative and judicial execution after the entry into force of the law, not one day after the entry into force of that law. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Amendment 14 is of a more precise nature as regards the references to Article. 62b § Point 1 (1). ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u75	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - the Polish Coalition to present our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration and some other laws, prints No. 3753 and 3806. The proposed law aims at simplifying the procedure for this procedure and speeding up the collection of public debts. The project introduces the principle of electronic transmission of implementing titles, allowing for their automatic verification and registration in the electronic system of the enforcement authority. The project assumes among others. the possibility for the creditor and the enforcement authority to request the data necessary for drawing up the status protocol. Organs, calling on the obliged person to submit the abovementioned document. statements will be required to instruct the debtor about the threat of criminal liability. The proposed provision will enable the creditor to obtain information on the assets of the debtor before the enforcement procedure is initiated. To date, the above-mentioned document has been drawn up. the minutes were the exclusive authority of the enforcement body. Another of the proposed changes concerns the need to include instructions for the obliged creditor to notify the creditor of the change of address of residence or residence, and also the enforcement authority, the head of the tax office, when the execution is initiated. Non-compliance with the above mentioned criteria. the obligation will result in the service of letters at the previous place of residence or residence. Despite its failure to do so, it will have legal effect. In addition, the notice generated automatically may not contain the signature of the authorised person. Thus, the new proposed amendments will derogate from the general principle of granting notice by signature of the authorised person. In the event of doubt as to the authenticity of the obliged person, he may exercise the right to bring charges in respect of administrative execution - inadmissibility of administrative execution. The changes covered by this project will also concern delegations for the adoption of a regulation setting out the model of the implementing title. This will result from the introduction into the Act of a new type of implementing title, i.e. the next implementing title. At the same time, the proposed provision was supplemented by guidelines on how to define the model of the implementing title - the possibility of unambiguous identification of the obliged person, the debtor and his spouse or the debtor in kind was introduced. In addition, the principle that the creditor shall indicate the last known address of the person's residence will be accepted in the implementing title itself. The change in the debtor's place of residence during the period between the issuing of the instrument and the initiation of the enforcement procedure significantly impeded its conduct. High Izbo! The changes proposed in the proposed proposal seem to be a step in the right direction. We welcome the introduction of the principle of electronic transmission of implementing titles, which will allow for their automatic verification and registration. It is also a good thing that the project eliminates the duplication of proceedings between the enforcement authority and the creditor.

As a result, the number of concessions for the exploration and recognition of shale gas decreased avalanchely, from 108 concessions between 2010 and 2013 to only 20 in 2017. The current shale gas deposits do not provide sufficient opportunities for their economic exploitation. Therefore, the legislative assumptions that the hydrocarbon tax will provide the State budget with an appropriate share of the profits from shale gas extraction are not met. The number of entities making declarations on specific hydrocarbon taxes is also falling successfully, with the vast majority of taxpayers showing losses in declarations. Therefore, the government presented a draft law, which in article. 1 introduces a legal solution for losing the power of the Act on special hydrocarbon tax. ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u109	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear guests! The draft law amending the pharmaceutical law is intended to ensure the application of the regulation so-called. Delegated Commission of the European Union 2016, which complements the 2001 Directive. the European Parliament and the Council. This amendment sets out the new tasks and competences of the chief pharmaceutical inspector and the voivodship pharmaceutical inspectors to supervise the performance of the obligations set out in the Commission Regulation. These obligations are imposed on the so-called. Marketing authorisation holder - manufacturers or importers of medicinal products, suppliers of pharmaceutical wholesalers and retailers of medicinal products. Here are some examples of changes made to the project: it was possible for the MAH to decide on the packaging of the medicinal product to provide protection against opening the packaging of the product from outside the catalogue provided for by the Regulation, it was decided that the placing of safeguards to verify the authenticity and identification of the medicinal products is a responsibility of manufacturers or importers, that the new competence of the Chief Pharmaceutical Inspector to supervise compliance by the MAH and other entities with data transmission and operations in the national system, etc. The Act will enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of certain provisions. Four minority proposals were submitted during committee meetings. The PiS Club is currently reporting three amendments at second reading, two of which I will briefly discuss. The first one provides for a reduction in the maximum amount of the fine for public pharmacies, hospital pharmacies, pharmacies and hospital pharmacy departments. This will be a reduction of up to five times. This is the consequence of the discussion at the committee meeting. Amendment 3 provides for an extension of the vacathio legis for the provisions on the prohibition of sub-contracting provided for by the Delegated Regulation, which I mentioned at the beginning. The PiS Club will vote in favour of the project, along with, of course, the amendments that I am now, at second reading, handing over to your marshal. Thank you very much.

I have the honour to present the position of the Parliament's Committee of Investigation to examine the regularity and legality of actions and the occurrence of negligence and omissions of public authorities and institutions in ensuring the revenue of the Treasury in respect of the tax on goods and services and excise duties in the period from December 2007 to November 2015. This report is a thick book with almost 500 pages, so I'll be able to only signal its main threads. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a very sad book. It is a book describing the history of how elites, how the power ruling Poland tragically failed Poles, in a tragic way failed to fulfil its responsibilities, and the Polish state in an extremely important area, which is the collection of danin, was defeated. Since ancient times, these were basic elements of the state: territory, monopoly on violence, ability to collect tributes from this territory. The Polish State in this period examined by the committee from this elementary, basic, state-constituted obligation was not able to fulfil itself. There are numerous reports about it - they said at that time, which is extremely important, which should not be a surprise to anyone, this knowledge was universal - reports from the European Commission prepared for the European Commission by various independent institutions, reports from Polish analytical organizations, after the time also of the Polish Ministry of Finance, because during the period under consideration the Ministry of Finance of this phenomenon did not investigate and was largely unaware of it, at least at the official level, although as I say, all the reasons for being aware of the contracts were. At a time when this scale of fraud, tax fraud and other factors affecting the expansion of the VAT gap increased, i.e. the difference between what should affect the budget and what actually affected it, this phenomenon had a few hundred percent scale, increased by several hundred percent. This gap at the beginning, in 2007, has been very low since the beginning of the period considered, one of the lowest in Europe, about 8% According to estimates, in a few years it reached a level of 25 So it's one of the record ones in Europe. There were several European countries where there was a larger gap or a similar level, but they were countries where this was due to such structural, long-term reasons and where it did not change much. Poland is the only phenomenon where the calendar of increase of fraud, increase of fraud, increase of VAT gap so simply overlapped on the calendar of who was in power. Because power then, as I said before, in a catastrophic way did not even fulfil its responsibilities. We have identified a number of actions that were being undertaken at the time, which led to the fragmentation of the tax system, which led to an increase in this leakage from the state budget. These few activities, which can be classified as somehow sealing, were always extremely late, for years late, very fragmented. And it was very often the case that when one gate, one gap was closed even a little bit, it was sometimes opened in the same act. Finally, the state's capacity to prosecute economic crimes, organised economic crime in general, and this in particular referred to VAT fraud, has been dismantled and at least seriously weakened. Because one thing has to be said: this is not about the fact that someone does not pay the tax, that someone produces, trades, conducts business, should pay some tax, but does not pay it. No. The worst thing about this phenomenon was that there were organized criminal groups who were stealing money from the state budget, which did not carry out any economic activity or was only such a covert, fictional character, mainly about falsifying documents creating a fictional economic turnover, in order to finally extort, i.e. stealing live cash from the state budget. Just as an example of such sealing activities, it is a matter of introducing the right to quarterly clearing for newly registered entities. So the newly registered entity in the VAT carousel on the so-called. It was not until after a quarter that the pole had to submit any report for the first time, that it appeared in the field of vision of the tax authorities. Of course, it's natural to be late sometimes, calls to fill in. Really, the check came in after 5 and 6 months. It's enough time for the VAT carousel to extort tens of millions of zlotys, transfer. And in the end, the proverbial pillar, the homeless, was caught without wealth. The organizers of this carousel remained unpunished, during which time they had already registered further companies, further entities, extorted further tens of millions of PLN. Another example of this kind of fragmentation is the abolition of the obligation to lodge security in import procedures, contrary to the clear position of the Department of Taxation from Goods and Services in the Ministry of Finance, where the head of the department stated unambiguously - this note of the commission was preserved, the committee reached it - that this change posed a serious risk of increased crime, increased extortion. However, after a personal intervention by Vice Minister Kapica, these security features were abolished and it was not ensured that alternative, other effective safeguards were put in place. The result was that in this section, i.e. in the area of tax collected by customs, the revenue to the budget for 4 years decreased by PLN 18 billion. Another example of such fragmentation is the introduction of a reverse VAT obligation for foreign operators. What else is reversed VAT in particular commodity groups. It's also a very controversial move, but quality, at least by some experts qualified as a sealing system... The introduction of an entity for foreign entities, registered abroad, led to the fact that, in this course of economic trade, where VAT is still being charged, compensated, cleared, there was a moment when an entity would not charge it, and therefore there was an additional right to reimbursement and an additional moment where a disappearing taxpayer could appear. Returns paid by the Tax Office Warsaw-Śródmieście II to such entities for one year increased twice, from PLN 5 to PLN 10 billion. These are examples of defrosting activities. Now the delays in sealing. The main actions that are presented as sealing the tax system in the years examined are the introduction of reversed VAT on individual goods. It is a very controversial instrument in itself. On a budget scale, however, what did he lead to? You could say nothing, I mean. year after year, the scale of extortion grew. It can be classified as helping the industry somehow. Entrepreneurs in a given industry were breathing with relief, because fraudsters, the mafia were pushed out of the industry, but it was pushed into other industries simply and the scale of fraud on the scale of the entire budget was still growing. Little was done so slowly, with years of delay, that for years they were still able to extort in a given criminal commodity, and then move on to another commodity. In the documents of the committee, in the documents examined, we have entire litas, entire series, dozens of letters from entrepreneurs, industry organizations to all possible institutions, with the Ministry of Finance at the head, including the Prime Minister, including law enforcement authorities. These industry organizations have shown in black and white what is the plague of extortion in their industries. Like I said, it took years to react. Just as an example, the first reversed VAT on scrap metal - already in 2009 was the law in the finance department, and it was not implemented until 2011. And yet, which is also significant for these pseudo-automating activities, it forgot to take into account the legal definition of scrap metal, which made it necessary to slightly change the name of the product on the invoice so that it could continue in an undistorted way. Fuels, hot stuff lately. As we know, on average 600 tanks with illegal fuel entered Poland annually. [[Voice from the room: I wonder where?]] Losses are over 20 million PLN per day for the budget. ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u215	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to present the Government's draft law on the settlement of double taxation disputes and the conclusion of previous price agreements, contained in Seym printing No 3788. The essence of the project is, firstly, the implementation of Directive 2017/1852 of the Council of the European Union of October 2017 on mechanisms for resolving disputes concerning double taxation in the European Union, secondly, the transfer to the draft law of the Tax Order on prior pricing arrangements, taking into account their simplification and improvement of the procedure for issuing such agreements, and, thirdly, the introduction of a cooperation programme, i.e. a new form of cooperation between strategic taxpayers and the head of the National Tax Administration based on mutual trust, understanding and transparency, which aims to ensure compliance with tax law with the support given by the Head of the National Tax Administration. The Act is part of the current package of solutions in income tax benefits for taxpayers. It improves existing double taxation dispute resolution procedures and introduces additional instruments that increase the certainty of taxpayers exposed to double taxation by other countries in achieving a positive solution to their problems within a certain timeframe. In particular, the most important proposed changes include, first, the introduction of a new mechanism to ensure the harmonisation of the rules for resolving disputes concerning double taxation of profits in the European Union, as well as a specific timeframe for tax authorities to take certain measures. For example, the decision to accept the case should take place within 6 months of the taxpayer's submission of the application and the procedure for mutual communication between States should be completed within 2 or 3 years of the start of the procedure. In addition, in the absence of an agreement between the States on whether or not the case is dealt with, the draft law provides for the possibility of setting up an arbitration panel at the request of a taxable person consisting of independent experts and who draws up an opinion on the adoption or settlement of the case. The draft law also introduces the possibility for a taxable person to assert his rights before a court, in particular in a situation of inaction of tax administrations, e.g. if the experts are not appointed to the advisory committee or if the taxable person is not informed within the time limit for the decision to be taken. Secondly, at the same time, the draft law organises double taxation procedures by concentrating existing measures under a single legal act: procedures under the Arbitration Convention for related parties operating in the European Union, and procedures under double taxation agreements that both companies and citizens use, e.g. Sailors or retirees. Thirdly, in the scope of the previous price agreements, the regulations already contained in the Tax Order were transferred to the draft law, extending them to the possibility for a foreign entity planning to start proceedings for a prior price agreement in Poland. This solution serves primarily the development of a socially responsible and willing business in Poland with tax obligations in proportion to the scale of the real activity carried out in Poland, which has a positive impact on the state budget. Fourthly, the draft law also provides the legal basis for the implementation of the cooperation programme by the National Tax Administration. This is intended to be a new form of voluntary cooperation between the National Tax Administration and strategic taxpayers based on mutual trust and transparency of conduct, which will reduce the cost of collecting taxes and reduce administrative burdens for entrepreneurs. The program will be addressed to the largest and reliable companies, i.e. with revenues above EUR 50 million and those whose internal tax supervision organisation ensures the correct implementation of tax obligations. These include corporate governance, internal control, audit, risk management, etc. It is planned that the Act will enter into force after 14 days from the date of publication in the Official Journal, except for parts of the provisions that will become applicable within other time limits, e.g. Cooperation programme - from 1 July 2020 Thank you very much.

Double taxation of profits in different countries has a negative impact on international economic activity and leads to a deterioration of business conditions and economic efficiency, thereby creating barriers to development in the common market. The lack of uniformity in the procedures for resolving double taxation disputes, including at EU level, in the context of progressive globalisation, changing tax rules, quantitative and valuable growth in cross-border economic operations may raise concerns about additional negative consequences for which procedures to protect taxpayers in the face of double taxation problems would be blocked. Thus, ladies and gentlemen, the Law and Justice Club will support this bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u219	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - Civic Coalition on the Government's draft law on the settlement of double taxation disputes and the conclusion of previous price agreements, print No 3788. The draft law introduces a new mechanism to ensure uniform rules for the settlement of disputes concerning double taxation of profits within the European Union. Double taxation of profits in different countries has a negative impact on international economic activity and leads to a deterioration of business conditions and economic efficiency, thereby creating barriers to development in the common market. The lack of uniformity of dispute resolution procedures, including at EU level, in the context of progressive globalisation is a negative phenomenon. The new mechanism to ensure the harmonisation of dispute resolution rules in this respect is included in the Council Directive of 10 October 2017 on mechanisms for resolving disputes concerning double taxation in the European Union. The Directive obliges all Member States to introduce the provisions necessary for its implementation. In addition, the draft law sets out the existing double taxation procedures for proceedings under the Convention on the Elimination of Double Taxation in the case of the correction of profits of affiliated enterprises and of proceedings under double taxation agreements, by concentrating them in a single legal act, which will affect their transparency and facilitate the choice of a specific measure. The provisions of the project are also transferred to the legal act before the previous price agreements concluded in the Tax Order, and they extend the regulations concerning previous price agreements by introducing the possibility for a foreign entity planning to initiate proceedings on this matter in Poland. The procedure for prior price agreements is widely available in many European Union and OECD countries, as well as in non-EU countries. The explanatory memorandum indicates a number of advantages resulting from the introduction of the possibility of prior price agreements, including in particular the possibility to agree on their terms of cooperation between the taxpayer and the tax authority. The issue of both double taxation of profits and prior pricing arrangements requires regulation. ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u226	Thank you very much, Minister. It was, of course, Minister Tadeusz Kościński. Thank you. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u227	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - the Polish Coalition to present our position on the governmental draft law on the settlement of double taxation disputes and the conclusion of previous price agreements, print No 3788. The aim of this project is to implement European Union law and concerns the introduction of a new dispute settlement procedure on double taxation of taxpayers' income and assets in the European Union, in accordance with the European Directive on Double Taxation Dispute Resolution Mechanisms (DRMs). The proposed provisions provide for the introduction of a new dispute resolution mechanism to avoid double taxation. This is a mechanism to ensure uniform rules for the settlement of disputes concerning double taxation of profits within the European Union, as provided for in Directive 2017/1852. It concerns matters arising from the interpretation and application of agreements and conventions providing for the elimination of double taxation of income or assets to which Poland is party. As is apparent from the assumptions that this mechanism will introduce a time and institutional framework and the principles under which the proposal initiating the mutual communication procedure will be adopted and examined, its implementation, the setting up of committees consisting of independent persons, and uniform guarantees for the resolution of the case, interaction with national proceedings and rules for derogation from the procedure will be provided. This mechanism will also benefit individuals and smaller entities within the meaning of Directive 2017/1852. The bill also provides for the possibility for a foreign entity planning to implement an investment in Poland to initiate the APA proceedings, which will create a national entity with which a foreign company will conclude controlled transactions. In that case, the decision will be issued for the national entity. According to the assumptions of the project, it is intended primarily to promote the development of a socially responsible and willing business in Poland with tax obligations in proportion to the scale of the real activity carried out in Poland. At the same time, it is only after the creation of a Polish entity that it is intended to reduce the number of such procedures in the APA case, which will not actually result in the start of investment in Poland. As of July 2020, taxpayers are expected to be able to join the cooperation programme. It follows from the assumption that the aim is to allow for a new form of cooperation between the strategic taxpayer and the head of the KAS based on mutual trust, understanding and transparency, which aims to ensure that the taxpayer complies with tax law with the support of the Head of the KAS. The programme is an innovative form of supervision of the KAS on the regularity of the fulfilment of tax obligations, carried out prior to the submission of the statement, focused on continuous identification or forecasting and solving tax problems in real time or before they occur. High Izbo! Double taxation leads to a deterioration of business conditions and economic efficiency, thereby creating barriers to development in the EU common market. It is therefore important to be a law so that the taxpayer can avoid double taxation in Poland and EU countries. The proposed solutions should have a positive impact on the activities of tax authorities by increasing the voluntary and proper performance of tax obligations by strategic entities. Taxpayers will also be able to reduce the tax risk of their business.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u237	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present a report by the Rules of Procedure Committee, the Parliamentary and Immunity Affairs Committee on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator and certain other laws. Marshal of the Sejm pursuant to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Bureau of the Sejm, sent the above draft law to the Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Committee for first reading on 2 September 2019. The draft law amending the Act on the implementation of the mandate of the Member and the Senator and certain other laws aims to ensure a greater transparency of public life than previously, by extending the scope of the property statements made by the persons listed in the bill. The proposal contained in the draft law is intended to confirm that the origins of the assets obtained do not raise doubts. Furthermore, the designer's intention is to ensure greater consistency in the current regulation in the area of property claims by public office holders. The Act of 9 May 1996 on the implementation of the mandate of the Member and the Senator proposes to extend the scope of the property statements made so far by Members and Senators to include, in addition to information on the personal property and personal property covered by the marital property, information on the personal property of the spouse and the personal property covered by the marital property of the own children, the children of the spouse, the adopted children and the persons living together with the person required to make the declaration. The proposed law assumes that, as is currently the case, declarations of property status of Members and Senators will be made public, but will not disclose information about address data, location of the property and information enabling the mobility of the person making the statement and the persons indicated in the statement, as well as personal data of the Member's spouse or senator, his own children, the spouse's children, adopted children and the person living together with the Member or the Senator. As in the case of declarations on the assets of Members and Senators, the draft law provides for amendments to the Law on the limitation of the conduct of business by persons performing public functions. It is therefore proposed to extend the scope of declarations of property made by managers of state positions and persons having certain other public functions relevant to the functioning of the State so as to include, in addition to information on the personal property and property covered by the marital property, information on the personal property of the spouse and the personal property and the property of the spouse and the property of the spouses, the children of the spouse, the adopted children and the persons living together with the person required to make the declaration. The draft law provides that the persons listed in the Act will be in addition to the statements made so far concerning the separate property and the property of the spouse and the personal property and the property of the spouse, the children of the spouse, the adopted children and the persons who are in common life with the person required to make the declaration. This will be a separate statement, the model of which will be set out in the annex to the proposed law. The draft law proposes that, in the case of statements relating to the property of the closest persons, the person receiving the declaration shall immediately forward one copy of the declaration to the head of the tax office competent for the place of residence of the person making the declaration. The draft law also proposes an extension of the catalogue of persons whose claims will be made public. The draft law also proposes the introduction of similar regulations for Members of the European Parliament. Following its first reading at its meeting on 5 September 2019, the Rules of Procedure, Parliament's and Immunity Affairs Committee, when eight amendments were tabled, of which six, two negative opinions were positive, and after considering the draft at its meeting on 11 September 2019, asks the High Sejm to accept the draft law submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u280	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on the commemoration of priest Władysław Gurgach on the 70th anniversary of death, prints 3724 and 3764. On 31 July 2019 the Marshal of the Sejm sent this draft resolution to the Committee on Culture and Media for first reading. It is a parliamentary project, a project of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. The representative of the applicants is Mrs Barbara Bartus. The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 29 August 2019, after introducing minor legislative and stylistic amendments, invites the High Sejm to approve the draft resolution submitted. Thank you very much to all Members who have worked in agreement on this project and to the staff of the Legislative Office. And I particularly thank Barbara Bartus for her commitment to this project. I present the draft resolution. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the commemoration of priest Władysław Gurgach on the 70th anniversary of his death. Father Władysław Gurgacz, ps. The father, Shem, was born on April 2, 1914 in Jabłonica Polska. He was educated in high school in Krosno and Korczyn, and then joined the Society of Jesus in the Old Village. In 1942, he was ordained priest. From 1945 he was a hospital chaplain in Gorlice and then - with the sisters of the maid in Krynica. During this time he became famous for supporting soldiers of the anti-Soviet uprising and pastoral care of them and for courageous anti-communist sermons and retreat teachings. He was uncompromising in matters of faith and strongly stigmatized the communist regime. In 1947, he joined a branch of the Polish Underground Independence Army and then became his chaplain in the sense of the duty of the soldier's surroundings spiritual care. He made sure that underground workers avoid situations where they would have to use weapons. The branch, whose base was Hala Łabowska in Sądecczyzna, survived until mid 1949, maintaining the sympathy and support of local residents. Father Gurgacz was captured by communists in Krakow in July 1949 after the failed acquisition of a branch held in a state bank. The priest did not attend, but he wanted to share the fate of those over whom he was spiritually caring. During the interrogation, Father Gurgacz opposed the accusations made against him and his conspiracy colleagues. He was not forced to testify incriminating members of the organization. In the last word he said: The current government is not the Polish government, but these are the usurpers sent by the Kremlin. The Military District Court in Cracow sentenced priest Władysław Gurgacz and soldiers of Stefan Balicki's branch, ps. Bylina, and Stanisława Szajna, ps. Eagle, death penalty. They were murdered on 14 September 1949 in a prison in Montelupie, Cracow. Their bodies were placed in Rakowicki's cemetery. On the 70th anniversary of the death of Father Władysław Gurgacz Sejm of the Republic of Poland, appreciating his merits for the Polish people and his contribution to the promotion of patriotism, he pays tribute to this great man and honors his memory. [[Applause]] As I have already mentioned, the Committee on Culture and Media, following 11 votes in favour, with one abstention, recommended that the High House adopt a resolution on the commemoration of Fr. Władysław Gurgach on the 70th anniversary of his death. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u292	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Environmental Law and the Trade Inspection Act. The draft is contained in print No 3522, and the committee's report is in print No 3784. The project was aimed at first reading in the above mentioned. committees on 12 June of this year. On 16 July, the joint committees carried out the first reading of the project and on 29 August the project was worked in detail. The result of the work of the two committees is the printing report No 3784 presented here. High Izbo! The public opinion calls for decisive action on air protection. It is obvious that we all want to live in a clean environment, maintain health and good health. The judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union relating to the omissions of previous governments in 2008 The current government's response to these challenges is the government's clean air programme, implemented since September 2018, involving serious public resources in the thermomodernisation of buildings and, above all, providing them with low-carbon energy sources. It is therefore necessary to do everything possible to ensure that this money is spent efficiently and intentionally so that dishonest traders cannot mislead the buyers of boilers by offering them those which do not comply with the applicable standards. It is estimated that about 200,000 are sold annually in Poland. solid fuel boilers having a rated thermal input of not more than 500 kW. Therefore, the provisions in this respect should be sufficiently tight and should allow effective verification of their application by the competent administrative authorities. High Izbo! On 1 August 2017, a regulation of the Minister for Development and Finance on requirements for solid fuel boilers was issued. They were issued on the basis of the provisions of Article. Article 4 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Environmental Law Act. This regulation entered into force on 1 October 2017 and limited the possibility of introducing boilers not complying with the standards of issue on the Polish market. Enforcement of the emission standards for solid fuel boilers is a problem not only in Poland but also in other countries of the European Union, since the case is governed by Commission Regulation (EU) 2015/1189 of 28 April 2015 implementing Directive 2009/125/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards ecodesign requirements for solid fuel boilers. This Commission regulation will be applied directly throughout the European Union from 1 January 2020 and the boilers will then be examined under the provisions of the Conformity Assessment System Act. High Izbo! The Polish regulation of 2017, already mentioned by me, as I said, limited the possibility of introducing boilers that did not meet the standards of emissions to our market, but did not eliminate them. It turns out that more than 20% of the boilers offered for sale - and this is data after the provisions of the 2017 Regulation have been introduced. - does not meet the emission requirements. As you can see, regulations usually try to keep up with the ingenuity of market players directed at avoiding these regulations. Even the amendment of the 2017 Regulation sealing its provisions has proved insufficient. Well, ladies and gentlemen, the freedom of movement of goods guaranteed by European Union law causes low-quality boilers to be imported into Poland from the countries of the European Union, Iceland and Norway, which are party to the Agreement on the European Economic Area, and Turkey. The restrictions resulting from our 2017 regulation apply only to Polish producers. Thus, without statutory provisions, imports of boilers which do not meet the standards cannot be reduced. It is necessary to introduce provisions that enable solid fuel boilers with a capacity of up to 500 kW to check compliance and to impose administrative penalties for non-compliance. High Izbo! This draft law contained in print No 3522 amends the Environmental Law and the Commercial Inspection Act. In environmental law, first of all, in art. 172a a definition of a solid fuel boiler with a rated thermal input of not more than 500 kW and a definition of the placing on the market of a solid fuel boiler with a rated thermal input of not more than 500 kW shall be introduced. Besides, in the following article. 168b provisions are introduced requiring the authorities of the Trade Inspection to carry out checks on operators placing solid fuel boilers on the market with the aforementioned power to comply with the provisions of the Regulation. Further provisions restrict, in fact, allow the placing on the market of solid fuel boilers which do not comply with the requirements of the Regulation from other countries of the European Union, from countries which are parties to the Agreement on the European Economic Area and Turkey. Provisions are also introduced to allow administrative traders to impose fines for the placing on the market of boilers not complying with the requirements of the Regulation. These are the changes contained in the article. 1 relating to the Environmental Law. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 There is one amendment to the Trade Inspection Act of 15 December 2000. The checks referred to in Article shall be added to the inspection obligations. Article 168b (1) (a) (ii) (ii) (ii) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii)) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) Art. 3, 4 and 5 contain transitional provisions and in Article. 6 a maximum limit is set for the expenditure of the State budget allocated to the implementation of the tasks arising from the present law in the years 2019 to 2028. The limit was set at 9 million PLN, i.e. 900 thousand PLN each. Each year, and this appropriation will be used to finance the cost of laboratory tests on controlled boilers. An additional 30 inspections are foreseen and, as the regulations say, two copies per inspection. Therefore, verification of documentation, verification of compliance with the required parameters, compliance of the data plate with laboratory test results, certificates, etc. will be carried out. Art. 7 of the draft provides that the law shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, except for a provision conferring a new wording of the Article. 172a Environmental law. There are certain definitions that I've already mentioned. This Article shall enter into force on 1 January 2020. High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u294	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! This will allow for effective checks on the compliance by operators placing solid fuel boilers on the market with a rated heat output of at least 500 kW with the requirements of the Regulation. In the event of failure to comply with these rules, those rules will allow the Trade Inspection bodies to restrict the placing on the market of equipment and allow administrative fines to be imposed. Since currently Polish producers are subject to requirements which are significantly stricter than foreign producers, the project introduces a provision to limit the placing on the market of solid fuel boilers in the country with a rated heat output of not more than 500 kW which do not meet the requirements of the Regulation, by all operators, including those from other countries. The combustion of fuels in high-carbon solid fuel boilers results in very harmful emissions of particulate matter and polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons to the health. Dust about the PM10 fraction causes cough, difficulty breathing and shortness of breath, especially during exercise. They contribute to increased risk of respiratory infections and exacerbations of symptoms of allergic diseases, e.g. asthma, hay fever and conjunctivitis. The introduction of the proposed provisions will allow for sealing of the control system on the application of the rules governing the marketing of solid fuel boilers, and this in turn will contribute to the effective enforcement of the rules aimed at reducing air pollution emissions from the municipal and domestic sectors. We proceed with the examination of item 20 of the agenda: First reading of the parliamentary draft law amending the excise duty Act (Print No 3813). I ask Mr Ireneusz Gain to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u328	Dear Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the group of Members of the Law and Justice, I shall submit and present a draft law amending the excise duty Act, print No 3813. In the Act of 6 December 2008 on excise duties, I request amendments to the Article. 105 on excise duty on passenger cars. Thus, the 2014 European Union Directive 94 imposed an obligation on Member States to develop a national framework for the development of alternative fuels infrastructure. This document sets out a strategy for the development of the market and infrastructure for alternative fuels in transport. In line with the requirements of Directive 94 of 2014 and the forecasts for the development of the alternative fuels market in Poland, the document sets targets for infrastructure for charging electric vehicles, filling with natural gas of vehicles and bunkering of marine natural gas. At the same time, the National Policy for the Development of Alternative Fuel Infrastructures identified complementary measures to stimulate the development of low-carbon transport not directly related to alternative fuels infrastructure, but fulfilling the requirement of at least partial replacement of oil-based energy sources. The document indicated the possibility of using alternative hybrid vehicles as one of the means of developing the market for alternative fuel vehicles, indicating a reduction in excise duty rates as a means of achieving this objective. This regulation implements the provisions of the National Policy Framework for the Development of Alternative Fuel Infrastructures in this respect. The Development of Electromobility in Poland, a strategic document adopted by the Council of Ministers on 16 March 2017, identified a number of barriers to the development of electromobility. It has been found that it is equally important in the context of supporting the construction of alternative fuels infrastructure to simultaneously promote the entire electromobility ecosystem, including in particular the creation of incentives for buyers of alternative fuels vehicles and manufacturers of such vehicles. This ecosystem will not function properly if, with the development of infrastructure and the increase in vehicle supply, promotion of their use is not increased, which will contribute to strengthening public awareness of the shift to low- and zero-carbon transport models. It is foreseen that the creation of an electromobility ecosystem will be a long-term process, which must take into account existing barriers in Poland. The identified problems arise not only from the current stage of market development, but in many respects from its actual shortcomings or too slow development in view of the needs and objectives of promoting the use of alternative fuels in transport. In addition, it is desirable to introduce additional instruments to reduce carbon monoxide emissions from the transport sector, with the need for this, inter alia. in Poland's energy policy by 2030 and European Union documents, a significant reduction in carbon monoxide emissions. The Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels introduced a catalogue of zero- and low-carbon vehicles related directly to the use of infrastructure prepared for these fuels, i.e. an electric vehicle, a hybrid vehicle, powered by hydrogen and powered by natural gas. A hybrid vehicle is defined as a vehicle within the meaning of Article. 2 point 33 of the Law of 20 June 1997 on road traffic with internal combustion and electric power, in which electricity is accumulated by connection to an external power source. According to the objective of this regulation, the definition is limited only to the plug-in model. However, the most accessible group of low-carbon vehicles on the Polish market are hybrid diesel-electric vehicles, where electricity is not accumulated by connecting to an external power source. Market data indicate that around 90,000 registered in Poland. this type of vehicle and that this segment of the market offers significant opportunities to significantly reduce emissions from car transport and further stimulate the gradual transition to a greater market share for zero-emission vehicles. Increasing the number of these vehicles in the transport sector will have a positive impact on speeding up the process of improving air quality in Poland, in particular in reducing emissions of nitrogen oxides and carbon. Therefore, the implementation of the limited support provided for these types of vehicles within the National Development Policy Framework and Alternative Fuel Infrastructures and the Development Plan for Electromobility in Poland is fully justified. The existing instruments in this area introduced by the electromobility Act provided for temporary exemption from excise duty on hybrid vehicles. The proposed provisions complement this instrument. They are introduced in order to accelerate the desired market developments and to adapt the appropriate measures to the characteristics of hybrid technologies. The powertrain in hybrid electric vehicles is predominantly based on high power electric units and associated high-heat efficiency engines to a lesser degree of compression and lower operating temperatures. Such a propulsion system ensures a significant reduction in emissions of harmful substances. The current regulation did not take into account the specificities of these drives and was a barrier to the development of the low-carbon drive market in this area. The proposed amendment is also implemented by the Economic Committee of the Council of Ministers on the measures necessary to take in view of the existence in a significant area of the country of high concentrations of air pollutants from January 2017, which included the use of tax mechanisms to introduce incentives for low-carbon transport, including a low excise duty rate for hybrid cars. The proposed provisions amend the excise duty Act of 6 December 2008. This is a 50% reduction in the rate of this tax for hybrid diesel-electric vehicles where electricity is not accumulated by connecting to an external power source. As regards motor vehicles within the meaning of Article. 2 point 33 of the Law of 20 June 1997 on road traffic, i.e. hybrid combustion-electric cars in which electricity is not accumulated by connection to an external source of power, with an engine capacity higher than 2000 cm3 but not higher than 3500 cm3, a reduced rate of 9,3% is proposed excise duty. In vehicles up to 2000 cm3 this rate is proposed at 1,55% the taxable amount of excise duty. This solution is consistent with the total exemption from the tax of zero-emission electric vehicles in force and maintained in force. According to the projector, the change will improve the use of low-carbon vehicles and help to promote and accelerate market transformation towards further development of the market for alternative fuel vehicles, and consequently to reduce emissions of harmful substances and improve air quality in Poland. The Act also has a limited impact on the small and medium-sized enterprises sector. The automotive industry is a strategic branch of Polish production due to its size. Automotive production facilities are located all over Poland. They are not only centres of local entrepreneurship but also generate many high-quality jobs both in the direct production of vehicles, parts and components, as well as in their surroundings. An important part of the production of automotive components is done by domestic small and medium-sized enterprises. Increasing the popularity of low-carbon drives can lead to the development of a new, innovative production and service environment. The inclusion of Polish producers in the production of components of modern alternative drives would contribute to technology transfer and increase the position of the domestic industry in global value chains. As regards the impact of the bill on the public finance sector, the precise impact of this regulation on the revenue of the state budget remains difficult to estimate. However, it is assumed that there is no impact on the revenue of the budget due to the shift in demand within the same vehicle lines. Hybrid combustion-electric vehicles in which electricity is not accumulated by connection to an external power source for technological reasons require a higher engine capacity with similar power and significantly reduced emissions. However, the current higher excise duty rate significantly reduces the demand for such cars despite much lower emission footprints. Therefore, it is expected that by lowering the excise duty on hybrid diesel-electric vehicles, despite higher prices, they will be as attractive in the long term as those with classic combustion vehicles due to reduced fuel consumption. This will shift market interest towards hybrid combustion-electric vehicles where electricity is not accumulated by connecting to an external source of power and equalising the revenue of the budget while reducing emissions of harmful substances in transport. My draft law is in line with European Union law. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Given that this draft law has a significant impact on the environment in such a way that the increase in the popularity of hybrid drives and the increase in the market share of such vehicles will result in a reduction in emissions, and that the solutions proposed in it are in line with European Union law and with the strategic documents I have mentioned, presented by the Council of Ministers, also with regard to national transport policy, and also with a plan for the development of electromobility in Poland, I very much ask the High House to support this draft law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u335	Thank you, sir. I ask Mr Andrzej Nobletta on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u336	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the parliamentary draft law amending the excise duty Act, print No 3813. Inspired by Members of the Law and Justice to submit a draft law on the amendment of excise duty was Directive 2014/94 of the European Union and the National Framework for the Development of Alternative Fuel Infrastructures for stimulating the development of low-carbon transport. That Directive required Member States to develop a national framework for the development of alternative fuels infrastructure. In line with the requirements of this Directive and with the expected development of the alternative fuel market in Poland, the document sets targets for infrastructure for charging electric vehicles and for filling vehicles with natural gas. The National Policy for the Development of Alternative Fuel Infrastructures identified the possibility of using hybrid electric vehicles as one of the means of developing the market for alternative fuels vehicles. The plan for the development of electromobility in Poland provides that the creation of an electromobility ecosystem will be a long-term process and must take into account existing barriers in Poland. The existing electromobility instruments provided for temporary exemption from excise duty for hybrid vehicles. The proposed provisions complement this instrument and are introduced to speed up the desired market changes. These provisions amend the excise duty Act of 6 December 2008, setting a 50% reduction in the rate of this tax for hybrid vehicles. This solution is consistent with the total tax exemption for zero-emission electric vehicles in force and maintained in force. According to the projectors, the change will improve the use of low-carbon vehicles and contribute to the dissemination and acceleration of market transformation towards the further development of the market for alternative fuel vehicles and consequently to the reduction of emissions of harmful substances and the improvement of air quality in Poland. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this draft law in further legislative work, reducing by 50% excise duty. Thank you very much.
