ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u344	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour and pleasure to present to you a draft law amending the Act on the informationization of activities of entities carrying out public tasks and other laws, which in principle introduces three major changes in addition to a number of other additions and changes in results. Firstly, the aim of the project is to create a legal basis for the operation of the system of state registers, in short the SRP, which will be a solution aimed at harmonising and defining the principles of operation of public registers. At the same time, the Minister responsible for IT is appointed as responsible for the functioning of the system. The system of state registers will contain only those registers for which the entities responsible will decide to join. This decision will be based on a voluntary basis. Therefore, we believe that there is an opportunity to address the problem of the lack of identity of data processed in different public registers. The project assumes that it is appropriate to recognise the PESEL register as a register containing data which, in relation to the same data collected in other public registers, will be treated as reference data. This Act will also require certain public entities to verify the data initially entered in relation to compliance with the data collected in the PESEL register. The obligation will apply to public registers operating using ICT systems and processing data of natural persons holding a PESEL number. The positive result of the verification will enable the data to be entered in the public register, while the negative result of the verification will be required by the introductory authority to explain immediately the facts concerning the detected non-compliance with the personal data contained in the PESEL register. The second change I would like to draw attention to is the introduction of a legal basis for the functioning of the contact data register, which will be kept in electronic form in a much safer and more secure way from the point of view of the protection of personal data compared to the traditional telephone book. In our view, this will also speed up the completion of administrative procedures and enable action to be taken to ensure that citizens and businesses are informed of their obligations and requirements. The contact data register will be implemented as a central register in the system of state registers, SRP. The following data will be collected in the register: PESEL number, name and, depending on the citizen's will, e-mail address and/or mobile phone number. The contact details will be provided in the register voluntarily. Their processing will take place only on the basis of the consent of the interested parties. The introduction of contact details, updates and deletions will be possible by the data subject himself, using an electronic service and through the public administration when carrying out matters by that authority for the person concerned. On the other hand, the provision of data to the entity belonging to the set of entities set out in the Act will be carried out by means of teletransmission devices after a single simplified application has been submitted by that entity after compliance with the conditions set out in the Act. And a third change I'd like to draw attention to. The draft law also introduces further facilitations dedicated to citizens by amending the Act on the Rights of the Civil Status Act, which will provide the legal basis for the provision of a dedicated electronic service enabling citizens to self-retrieve a copy of the Civil Status Act in electronic form directly from the Register of Civil Status. We plan that this service will be available not only to the person concerned but also to her spouse, children and parents. It should be stressed that, thanks to the changes proposed in the bill, citizens will be given the opportunity to take advantage of solutions, tools which will relieve them in their daily care of official matters, speed up and facilitate the handling of their affairs and help avoid the negative consequences resulting from the failure to settle the case or the timely handling of the case. In view of the above, I shall bring and ask the High House to pass this bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u354	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a bill on the modernisation of Industrial Property Rights. This project is a very important project for innovation policy, because industrial property law itself, the protection of industrial property, is an important instrument that shapes this policy. We believe that this amendment makes the Patent Office a stronger support for the development of technology and technology in Poland. The amendment follows the demands put forward in the White Paper on industrial property by the environment concerned and, of course, by the entrepreneurs themselves. If your marshal will allow me, I will briefly present the most important solutions that the bill introduces. Firstly, we are bringing the provisions of Industrial Property Rights closer to the relevant provisions contained in the European Patent Convention and the Washington Patent Cooperation Agreement, among others. by specifying the definition of the invention, specifying the requirement of uniformity of the application of the invention, introducing a restriction of the patent into the Polish legal order of the institution. A significant change is to increase the availability of legal assistance in the field of industrial property protection by introducing a solution whereby the party's proxy in proceedings before the Patent Office in matters of industrial designs and geographical indications may be, in addition to patent attorneys, lawyers or lawyers. We also extend the possibility of exemption from periodic fees, for the first period of protection, to industrial designs and trademarks. This is so important that for entrepreneurs at the initial stage of doing business all kinds of fees, tributes, burdens are important and can hinder the development of their business activities. We also amend the rules governing civil law claims, i.e. a claim for the protection of evidence relating to infringements of industrial property rights and so-called. an information claim, in such a way as to ensure the proper protection of the entitled while taking into account the rights of third parties who are not infringers of industrial property rights. An important change is also the introduction of the term of office of the President of the Patent Office of the Republic of Poland. The term of office is to last 5 years. The term of office is to be limited to two terms of office. We want to simplify the procedure related to rebuilding the competitive advantage of Polish entrepreneurs based on a strong brand and unique solutions. Modernising Industrial Property Rights by bringing national law closer to European and global standards means improving the functioning of this system for the eligible, including entrepreneurs themselves. This will enable them to obtain protection of exclusive rights in a simpler and faster way not only in Poland, but also from the European Patent Office and even other international patent offices. This allows our entrepreneurs to build competitive advantages based on a strong brand and unique technical and technological solutions, especially in the IT industry or in Polish design industries, which is of particular importance to us. The last point I wanted to draw attention to, which is linked to the previous one, is the question of a more predictable case law of the patent office. By bringing regulations closer to European law and the European Convention on the Grant of European Patents entitled, including entrepreneurs interested in acting exclusively on the Polish market, will be able to patent their inventions directly in the Patent Office of the Republic of Poland and not in the European office. As a result of these changes in patent regulations, the case law of the Polish Patent Office will be more predictable, as the departure from views presented by the European Patent Office will require additional justification. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Due to the importance of this project for Polish entrepreneurs, in order to increase the innovation of the Polish economy, I kindly ask you to adopt the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u356	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! The amendment of the Law of 30 June 2000 on Industrial Property Law, hereinafter referred to as the Law of 29 April 2004 on the Enforcement of Intellectual Property Rights, is the result of the need to eliminate the errors identified by the environment relating to industrial property in the implementation of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 29 April 2004 on the enforcement of intellectual property rights. The draft also contains a number of provisions adapting to the Convention on the granting of European patents. As regards the amendments related to the approximation of national provisions to the provisions of the Convention on the granting of European Patents, [[European Patent Convention]] drawn up in Munich on 5 October 1973, and the Patent Cooperation Agreement, drawn up in Washington on 19 June 1970, this amendment implements into the Polish legal order certain legal institutions, known under the Convention on the granting of European patents, in particular adapts the conditions for obtaining patent protection and introduces the right to restrict the patent by amending patent reservations. The draft amendment of the Act does not contain provisions laying down the conditions for the pursuit of economic activities and therefore it is not necessary to apply the provisions of Resolution No 20 of the Council of Ministers of 18 February 2014 on recommendations for the harmonisation of the time limits for the entry into force of certain normative acts. The draft does not contain technical provisions within the meaning of the Council of Ministers Regulation of 23 December 2002 on the functioning of the national system of notification of standards and legal acts. The proposed regulation is in line with European Union law. Please give us a positive opinion and adopt this bill. Thank you, Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u446	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Five amendments were tabled during the second reading of the Act amending the Environmental Law and the Commercial Inspection Act. The Committee on the Economy and the Committee on the Environment have examined these amendments. Amendment 2 introduces an exemption from personal income tax, i.e. from PIT, for all grants and other non-refundable benefits received from national and voivodship environmental funds for individual environmental projects. The beneficiaries of the Clean Air scheme already benefit from such exemption in accordance with the amendment made in November of the previous year. Now, the income tax exemption will benefit individuals, farms, companies, small plants that set up photovoltaic micro-installations, e.g. as part of my current programme. Amendments 3 and 4 require the use of system heat where there are technical conditions to deliver it, which is better for the air condition, because heating plants must use industrial filters, so they do not pollute air and the scale effect allows to reduce the costs of supplied heat. There will be no obligation to connect to the system heat when a low emission individual heat source is installed or when the price of the system heat is uncompetitive. Amendment 5 in turn introduces a reduced VAT rate, i.e. 8% instead of 23% , for the supply and construction of a micro-installation of RES, which is functionally related to the building, regardless of whether this micro-installation is located in the building's brills or outside of it. This is an obvious elimination of the nonsense of varying VAT rates depending on the location of the micro-installation. Amendment 1 concerns the title of the Act and is the consequence of all the amendments under discussion from 2 to 5. High Izbo! All amendments received a strong majority recommendation from the combined economic and environmental committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u472	Mr. Chairman, maybe a moment... Ladies and gentlemen, I am so sorry. Please move the calls to the cocksuckers. Let's respect each other. [[Voice from the courtroom: Yes, Marshal.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u473	I will repeat that 2018 was the first year of operation of the National Judicial Council appointed according to the new procedure. Only that it is effectively 8 months: from May to December 2018, because for the first 4 months of the year the National Council of the Courts was still in its previous composition. This period of work can be summarised with several basic figures. The Council shall be a collegial body, acting at meetings. The meetings shall be held in accordance with the plan. There were 17 meetings in 2018. At these meetings, 1232 candidates were assessed as judge or asessor and asked the President of the Republic of Poland to appoint 322 judges or assessors. Currently, this number already exceeds 600 people, but in 2018 there were 322 candidates. Five meetings of the previous Council - 21 resolutions over a period of 3 months. On the other hand, the next meeting of the Council in its new composition brought 486 resolutions for 8 months. I'm talking about the so-called. resolutions on personal matters, which means 54 resolutions per month. This is more resolutions than the previous Council has taken over a period of 3 months. A total of 1232 candidates were evaluated. It is worth noting that most of them were judges, but there was a trend - pointing to opening up to other environments, as among the 1232 candidates we also dealt with 114 lawyers, 117 legal advisers, 52 people from different backgrounds: government officials, tax advisors and academics. [[You'll return all the payments with interest.]] Applications for appointment mainly concerned district courts, i.e. The candidates came primarily from regional courts. One can see here a correlation between some kind of unblocking of applications from these courts and the fact that there is a very low representation of regional judges in the previous term of office of the judicial council. For 30 years, there have been three regional judges in each composition of the National Judicial Council, which means that there were entire terms, during which no regional judge was a member of the National Judicial Council. Another point that I would like to draw attention to, because it has raised some interest in the public and led to misunderstandings, is the question presented on page 43 of this report on the so-called. Revocations. Well, candidates apply for competitions, but they can withdraw their application until the end of this competition. This withdrawal is purely technical and is committed to improving its position in other competitions to which these candidates may apply. On the other hand, at the beginning of the Council's action in 2018 - I am talking about the period since May 2018 - these purely technical withdrawals were presented as an expression of boycott of competitions conducted by the Council or boycott of the Council as such. Mr Dariusz Zawistowski of the Supreme Court, the President of the Civil Chamber of this court and the Chairman of the National Judicial Council for a time, also spoke on this subject. He was also in favour of such a position presented in the media, such interpretation, but the number of withdrawals is not entirely different from the number of withdrawals we had in the work of previous compositions of the National Judicial Council, which leads to the conclusion that there is no boycott here, but it is completely technical. For the record, on average, these withdrawals are 23 per month, and for one sitting there will be around 14. Since 486 resolutions on personal matters received 57 appeals, some were recognised by the Supreme Court. In this respect, 14 judgments were delivered, three appeals dismissed, two dismissed, six cases repealed and cases re-examined, which means that the stability of the decisions of the national council is around 60%. and should be assessed as an average. On the other hand, the Supreme Court also issued 15 provisions on the suspension of the proceedings concerning the appeals lodged or on the grounds of adjournment for the submission of questions, asking questions for a preliminary ruling to the Court of Justice of the European Union. One of the powers of the National Judicial Council is the issue of switching or moving judges to rest. In this regard, 43 resolutions were passed in 2018, 43 judges retired, eight cases refused to be retiring, while it is worth noting that no Supreme Court judge and no Supreme Court judge were retiring. In this respect, one appeal was lodged and dismissed by the Supreme Court. It is also the responsibility of the National Courts Council to evaluate draft normative acts, if they are, in general, related to the functioning of the judiciary. Such projects were received 134, requests for an opinion were dealt with in 119 cases, 15 over the following year and have certainly already been dealt with. Of these 134, the majority concerned draft laws - 78, 50 concerned regulations, and the others concerned one Senate resolution, one order of the Senate marshal, one notice of the Sejm marshal and two regulations of the European Parliament. The most important in this respect were draft amendments to the Codes in the number 27, a detailed breakdown is presented in tab 64. The National Judicial Council also has the possibility to present general positions on various matters relating to the functioning of the judiciary. In this respect, the Council adopted its position in 17 cases, in three cases the Presidency. The Bureau's positions concerned issues of a technical nature. In 2018, in one case, the Council exercised the power laid down in Article. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 2 of the Constitution, namely that it submitted one application to the Constitutional Tribunal on the basis of a resolution of 22 November 2018. The judgment was delivered on 21 March 2019. ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u477	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The information provided by the National Judicial Council is reliable in terms of its scope. As regards the normative changes that were carried out in 2018, the Sejm Act of 8 December 2017, which entered into force on 17 January 2018, first of all changed the choice of members of the National Judicial Council from among judges. The possibility of submitting candidates was granted to a group of at least 2 000. citizens or a group of at least 25 retired judges. Finally, the election was awarded to the Sejm, which elects 15 judges from among the candidates submitted for a joint four-year term by a qualified majority of 3/5 votes in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of Members. The Act of 10 May 2018 amended the appointment procedure for the position of judicial assailant in the General Court, restored the model in which the President of the Republic of Poland, and not the Minister of Justice, appoints the court assessors in the General Courts. By Law of 20 July 2018, the Law on the System of General Courts amended the procedure for proceedings before the National Courts Council. Finally, the last amendment of 21 November 2018, where the National Council has obtained powers to present to the President of the Republic a declaration of the will of the Supreme Court and of the Supreme Administrative Court Judges, will remain at rest under Article. 2 (2) 1 presented act. The National Council is now composed of the first President of the Supreme Court, the Minister of Justice, the President of the NSA, the President of the President, 15 members elected from among the general courts, four members elected by the Sejm from among the Members and two members elected by the Senate. It must be said that it is good that the National Courts Council is currently composed of judges of regional courts. This practice has not been used before. It is also a good thing that more and more willing persons from outside the direct judicial profession apply for the judicial profession. They're lawyers, counselors... ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u519	Dear Marshal! Dear Messengers, Dear Members, Honourable Guests! I would like to present once again a report on the activities of the Ombudsman and information on the state of compliance with human rights and freedoms in the Republic of Poland. I would like to begin by thanking my colleagues and colleagues for another year of hard work and hard work. The deadline for submitting the annual Ombudsman's information was this year at the summit of the election campaign. So let me start by asking you. As a spokesman, I would not like to participate in the election campaign or in the pre-election game. For me, the only thing that matters is how human rights and civil rights and freedoms are protected and implemented. What I will say, of course, refers to the current power, because that is the role of the Ombudsman, but it would always refer to any other one that will be elected. Therefore, please do not treat me as a competitor or an ally in the election. Please also do not use the annual information I submit for instrumental purposes, because I am a government official who performs his constitutional tasks within the scope of his 5-year term of office, and I do so in the interests of all citizens, including all voters. I never ask people who report their problems, their views, I'm interested in the problems they come with, and solving these problems is the essence of the Ombudsman's work. Problems, I stress, not mine, not imaginary, not abstract, but from human complaints and feelings of harm. So I ask that you treat them in a truly responsible, worthy way. And I say this because of the situation that took place at yesterday's session of the Parliament's Committee on Justice and Human Rights. I would like to stress that some of the legislative changes that have been adopted recently were the result of the Advocate General's speeches. These were among others. Act of 30 August this year. on the amendment of the Act on recognition of invalid judgments handed down against persons repressed for activities for the independent existence of the Polish State, the Act of 19 July this year. the special rights of persons affected in the event of exhaustion of the guarantee sum established on the basis of the provisions in force before 1 January 2006 or the Act on Accessibility to persons with special needs, which provides for additional obligations on architectural, digital and communication accessibility. I am pleased that in this way some of the Ombudsman's demands have been met. I would also like to stress that, as part of my work, I am trying to work with all the other spokespersons, as well as with the Supreme Chamber of Control, and to add that the spokesperson for the rights of the child Marek Michalak was honoured by me with a badge of honour for his merit in protecting human rights for his outstanding 10-year service to the Republic of Poland. [[Applause]] Dear Marshal! Dear Messengers, Dear Members, The Ombudsman's report is of particular importance from the point of view of Article. 202 constitutions, because this is not only information about the Ombudsman's activities, but above all information about the state of compliance with civil rights and freedoms. This information obligation is of particular importance because it is a form of transmission of observations and opinions by the Ombudsman on whether human rights are respected in Poland, but it is also a form of transmission of this information to the public. It is worth adding that, of course, citizens have, in accordance with the Constitution, the right to lodge complaints with the Ombudsman, which they actively benefit from. The number of complaints remains broadly the same as in previous years. The same is true for other contacts, visitors, and phone contacts. Of course, it is impossible to present the entire report of the Ombudsman, as the report has several hundred pages. We really are trying to write quite cost-effectively about what we are doing and at the same time give away the essence of the most important problems. On the other hand, I would like to focus on the presentation of 15 issues, 15 specific issues which require a solution in Poland. These are the topics that I constantly insist on and which, unfortunately, do not always meet with the right response. The first problem concerns the situation of persons with disabilities and their carers. We still have a lot to do with the reform of the disability case law system. It is absolutely necessary to reform legal institutions that support people with disabilities in decision-making and, in this context, to abolish incapacity. This is what the Convention on the Protection of the Rights of Persons with Disabilities requires of us. The Polish government was also called upon to do this by the relevant UN committee. But I still - and I regret this - the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal on the guardians of persons with disabilities is not enforced. This sentence was passed over five years ago. More than 5 years ago, the Constitutional Tribunal concluded that the situation of carers cannot be differentiated according to the time when the children's disability arose and a large proportion of carers were left without the right to a higher benefit. We also have problems in deinstitutionalising the care of people with disabilities. The point is that these people can stay as long as possible in places that are best suited to their needs, not in big piers, big hospitals and social services. There are also a number of complaints from parents of students with disabilities who often describe dramatic conditions for teaching their children. And these complaints seem to confirm that the idea of inclusive education is not implemented in practice, and students with disabilities are pushed to the margin of the education system. The second problem I would like to mention is the situation at the National Centre for the Prevention of Dissocial Behaviour in Gostynin. There are now 72 people there, with 60 places planned. The number of patients is constantly increasing. On the other hand, what is lacking is simply the world's usual proper regulation of the rights of those who reside there. The court's approach to these cases also raises a number of concerns, particularly the use of a safeguard procedure, in principle a civil procedure to deprive people of freedom. And I ask the question: Do we really have to wait for the European Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg to judge these matters so that we can begin to take this seriously at all? This law, on the basis of which the centre in Gostynin was founded, has many legal gaps. It should be completely revised, and the philosophy of the centre itself should be changed. I would also like to address the problem of violence in police stations. We all know the Igor Stachowiak case, but there are other cases that are identified by my office. I care about systemic changes, in particular to guarantee real access to legal assistance as soon as it is stopped. There is also no precise definition of torture in the Penal Code, which results in torture being often treated merely as a breach of powers. I would like to point out that right now in Poland is visiting the ad hoc Committee on the Prevention of Torture of the Council of Europe. This is the first visit of the committee, which is organised on an ad hoc basis. As a general rule, this committee only planned to appear in Poland in 2021, but it came to the conclusion that the situation of violence by police officers is so worrying that it requires an additional extra programme visit. The problem in Poland is also the lack of effective fight against hate crimes, regulated by Article. 256 and Article 4 (1) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council 257 Penal Code. I'd expect a lot more involvement of the D.A.'s office in this kind of thing. I am concerned about the results of the research that we have carried out with the OSCE, which shows that only 5% of such crimes are reported to law enforcement authorities. Why? Since the victims do not have confidence in the institutions, they do not believe that something will happen to these matters, that the authorities of the state will act accordingly. A lot of complaints that go to the Ombudsman's office are about environmental protection. The issue of measles is still not regulated. There's a smog problem. The new phenomenon is landfills of trash, their arson, lack of support for municipalities in the disposal of trash. We look forward to many different solutions. The government promised to pass a law on livestock investments, but it did not even go to the Sejm. Another problem is the exercise of parental rights. I try to work with my father's environment, listen to the voice of mother's associations, I realize how much these environments are antagonized at present, and the basis for these antagonisms is the conflict in which the child remains most often. Family law does not keep pace with the current problems faced by families in the bipolar conflict, which prevents the state from reacting effectively and supporting them. Good law and the efficiency of judicial proceedings should be the dominant criteria that we should follow in order to resolve these problems. I regret that the law implementing the so-called law has not been passed. Alimony immediately. We were investigating her project in this room, and I supported this project because I thought he had a chance to put an end to one of the conflicts between his parents. Unfortunately, it wasn't passed. Another thing is the unsolved problem of the owners of housing books. This is the problem that I once again hoped would be resolved by the implementation of the "Housing+" programme, such were the announcements from the Ministry of Infrastructure. We still have a whole group of housebook holders who can't benefit from the guarantee premiums they have. Another problem is access to social housing as well as the quality of social housing. In Poland there is also a lack of a systematic approach to the problems of people in the homeless crisis. We need better coordination of state action, greater determination, and mental change in the perception of homeless people. My experience with numerous regional trips also shows that there is a problem of transport exclusion. It is not just a lack of public transport, but a related problem with the right to education, health, access to culture, the exercise of senior rights. Again: Should we read further reports, further books by Charles Trammer, Olga Gitkiewicz, reports by the Jagiellonian Club before you react? The State should develop a strategy to address this problem. Of course, health problems are a major issue. The situation in hospital emergency services, doctor queues, lack of doctors and nurses of certain specialties, underfunding of health services, lack of access to medicines, including those imported by means of final import, the situation in geriatrics and child psychiatry - these are all very serious problems. I would like to draw attention to one thing. In 2018, 98 thousand called the youth trust phone. children and adolescents. And now: we know that there are problems that young people contact and yet we do not see the great crisis in child psychiatry. We talk so much about family support, and we forget about real interest in matters and problems of children in families. Of course, when we talk about children, the Ombudsman deals with issues related to education reform. We warned about the consequences of the accumulation of the yearbooks, the problems of recruiting for secondary schools, and that this could lead to a deterioration in the quality of teaching. However, complaints to the office also indicate that there is a need for systematic anti-discrimination education. I am convinced that school can be a place where young people will be taught respect for others without violating the right of parents to raise them according to their own beliefs. As regards social rights, from the Ombudsman's point of view, a great deal of complaints concern the situation of citizens following the adoption of the Act of 16 December 2016, so-called. a repressive law that applied collective responsibility mechanisms. This is no longer just a matter of this law and what it has introduced, but also a question of real access to the court and the fact that because of the underfinancing of institutions, which are to deal with, for example, the transfer of complaints to courts, there is a considerable length of time in the proceedings. Many people in the country also deal with consumer rights, especially those who have taken loans denominated in foreign currencies. Here, unfortunately, we can only count on the courts that give us a chance to settle these cases. The Court of Justice of the European Union also comes to the aid. For example, today a valid judgment on repayment of credit costs in the case of early repayment - the Lexitor case. We are waiting for an even more important judgment of the Court on the Dziubak State, which will be important for many borrowers. Finally, surveillance is also a problem from the point of view of rights. That is not true, it is not acceptable to say that it is unacceptable for politicians to say that honest citizens have nothing to fear because public authorities cannot collect, collect and share information about citizens other than is necessary in a democratic state. It is a fundamental right to privacy, but it is also a matter of freedom of communication, journalistic secrecy or attorney-client privilege. I regret that the opinion of the Venice Commission has not been taken into account in any way, which indicates that our law, the Law on Police and Telecommunications Law contains many shortcomings in the provision and use by the authorities of telecommunications data, billing and IT data, and that no action is being taken to create an independent system of supervision of the activities of special services in accordance with recommendations. Dear Marshal! Dear Members, A few questions. Mr Majka's question on the article. 54 constitutions. Art. 54 This is a rule that says not only that we can say certain things, but also that as citizens and representatives of society, society also has the right to obtain information. Please note that it is not only about disseminating information but also about obtaining information. In our legal system, we have certain institutions, such as public media, but also the Polish Press Agency, which should inform about all important events in the public interest. They should not discriminate, they should not restrict access to information, all relevant events should be kept informed on an ongoing basis. Of course, the question arises as to whether this always happens. I do not know this situation with the PAP, which you spoke about, but, of course, when it comes to public media, I have often intervened and pointed out that perhaps the communication of information is not as it should be. ParlaMint-PL_2019-09-11-sejm-86-1.u583	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The 8th term of office of the Sejm, during which there have been major changes in the education system, especially in the field of school system. Here you can use a comparison to renovate the house. It was rebuilt, generating some nuisance, as usual, but the time for finishing and home appliances has come again. Similarly, there are further challenges to education arising from the need to improve the reform and the conditions resulting from dynamic changes, particularly in the economic and social spheres. It is necessary to redefine a coherent educational and educational concept of the Polish school. Therefore, it is important to start a calm work on a comprehensive integrated programme basis, including pre-school education and a full cycle of primary and secondary education. The new basis developed should be closely linked to the revised framework curricula. This coherent arrangement should ensure inter-subject integration, greater flexibility in the organisation of school work and the creation of conditions for individual work with an able and supportive student. It is essential to develop a coherent catalogue of knowledge, competences and skills for pupils, which will be the aim of the educational and educational process. Particular attention should be paid to education in the field of natural subjects and information technology, which is the basis for vocational training, and to the development of innovative and entrepreneurial attitudes. The basic condition for success of the new teaching concept is good substantive and methodological preparation of teachers. Changes in the education and improvement system are needed, as the changes so far resulting from, for example, the reform of higher education appear to be insufficient. Particular attention should be paid to developing broadly understood pedagogical skills. Another key condition for building a good Polish school is to ensure stable organizational conditions. It is therefore necessary to develop a coherent system for financing the education system in connection with economic development. Under this system it is necessary to ensure an adequate level of remuneration for teachers and non-pedagogical workers. The revised remuneration system should closely link the level of remuneration with the quality of work and the scope of tasks carried out by the teacher concerned. Furthermore, it is extremely important to call for a fundamental change in pedagogical supervision aimed at assessing the outcomes of education and education. They allowed schools to improve the educational and educational process. It guaranteed the acquisition of property rights of the premises by transforming residential and cooperative housing at the cost of construction. Since the co-operatives, making their own contribution and repaying the loan, were merely to supplement the construction cost by the amount of the budget subsidy which, after the denomination, constituted a small sum, millions of co-operatives became owners of the apartments under very favourable conditions.
